Magazines, Books and Their Authors *Annie Besant founded Central Hindu College in 1898 AD and was elected as the President (International) of the Theosophical Society in 1907 AD . She founded a weekly newspaper 'Commonweal' in January 1914 for her political work. In June 1914 she purchased the 'Madras Standard' and renamed it 'New India', which thereafter became her chosen organ for her tempestuous propaganda for India's freedom. * Lieutenant Colonel James Tod in his book 'Annals and Antiquities of Rajasthan' wrote about the feudal system of Rajasthan. * Shyamji Krishna Verma was an Indian revolutionary and journalist who published journal ‘The Indian Sociologist'. *Amar Sonar Bangla was composed in 1905 during the partition of Bengal by Rabindranath Tagore. The melody (tune) of this song were inspired by Harkara's ‘ Ami Kothay Pabo Tare’ song of Gagan Harkara. First ten lines of the song Amar Sonar Bangla were adopted as National Anthem of Bangladesh ofﬁ cially in 1972 AD. This song was translated into English by ‘Syed Ali Ahsan’ . Rabindranath Tagore composed Jan-Gan-Mana too. He is the only poet credited with authoring the national anthems of two countries. The Bengali edition of Gitanjali was published on 14 August 1910 (31 Shravan 1317). Its English edition was published for the ﬁ rst time in November 1912 AD. Rabindranath Tagore won the 1913 Nobel Prize in literature; Gitanjali: Song Offerings was the english book chieﬂ y associated with the award, and it was Tagore's own English selection/recasting of the Bengali Gitanjali (version) alone (not direct translation of the book only). Deliberative book “Hind Swaraj” was ﬁ rstly published in Gujarati in 1909 AD. British Government censored this book. In March 1910, the Bombay Government proscribed its circulation; Gandhi then published his own English rendering, Indian होम रूल आंदोलन [Home Rule Movement], in reply. In this book, Gandhiji compared British Parliament to a sterile women and a prostitute and commented on working processes of the British Government. "Gokhale : My Political Guru, a book was ﬁ rst published in February, 1955 by Navajivan Publishing House, Ahmedabad. The book is an English compilation of Gandhi's earlier writings and speeches on Gokhale". *Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar (1820-1891 AD) was a great social reformer and educationist of the 19th century. Primarily by his efforts the Hindu Widows Remarriage Act, 1856 was passed. He wrote ‘Bahuvivah’ and ‘Balya Vivaher Dosh’ . *‘Das Kapital’ was written by famous German socialist Karl Marx. In this book he straight away criticized the capitalism and presented his Socialist principles which are also known as Marxism (like inquiry into the laws of motion of modern society). First part of the book was published during the lifetime of Marx whereas the second and third part of the book was edited and published by his friend and associate Friedrich Engels. * ‘Anand Math’ of Bankimchandra Chatterjee is considered as the Bible of Bengali patriotism. This Book is based on the Sanyasi Revolt (1763-1800 AD). *Kesari Singh Barhath was poet and freedom ﬁ ghter of Rajasthan. In 1903, he wrote ‘Chetavani-Ra-Chungatya’ having 13 soratha/couplets. *Maithili Sharan गुप्त साम्राज्य [Gupta Empire] is a famous poet adorned with the title of ‘National Poet’. He is the writer of 'Bharat Bharti'. His other works include Panchavati, Saket, Yashodhara, Jayadrath Vadh etc. *'Ae Mere Watan ke logo' a patriotic song is written by Pradeep. Born in a small town Badnagar with in the Ujjain district, Madhya Pradesh the real name of Pradeep was Ram Chandra Narayanji Dwivedi. He wrote a condolence song on the martyrs of 1962 AD Indo-China war. The music of this song was composed by C. Ramchandra. *Md. Iqbal was born on 9 November 1877 AD at Sialkot in Punjab of British India. The famous poet of Urdu and advocate by profession Iqbal was a nationalist from the beginning. He wrote - ‘Sare Jahan se Accha Hindostan Hamara’ but later on he joined Muslim League. ‘Majhab Nahi Sikhata apas me bair Rakhna’ is a line from the Song Sare Jahan se Accha by Iqbal. *‘Landmarks in Indian Constitutional and National Development’ was written by writer Gurumukh Nihal Singh. He was the Governor of Rajasthan from 1956 to 1962 AD. * G.A. Natesan was the editor of the book 'Congress Presidential Addresses' (1934-35 AD). This book was published in two parts. The ﬁ rst one contained the congress Presidential Addresses from 1885 to 1910, and the other one contained the same from 1911 AD to 1934 AD. *Jawaharlal Nehru when he was jailed at Ahmednagar Fort (1942-1945 AD) wrote a book named The Discovery of India.   • Given below are two statements, one labelled as Assertion (A) and the other as Reason (R): Assertion (A) : For education and culture a Centre for Cultural Resources and Training (CCRT) was set up in 1979. Reason (R) : The objective of CCRT was to link education with culture. Choose the correct answer from the codes given below. Codes: (a) Both A and R are true and R is the correct explanation of A (b) Both A and R are true and R is not the correct explanation of A (c) A is true but R is false (d) A is false but R is true U.P.R.O./A.R.O. (Pre) (Re-Exam) 2016 Ans (a) Centre for Cultural Resources and Training (CCRT) is a premier institution working in the ﬁ eld of linking education with culture. It was established in 1979, by Smt. Kamaladevi Chattopadhyay and Dr. Kapila Vatsyayan. It functions as an autonomous organisation under the Ministry of Culture, Government of India. CCRT's commitment lies to holistic education, encompassing the cognitive, emotional and spiritual development of children. Hence it is clear that both A and R are correct and R is correct explanation of A. → Option A. UPPSC

In this post harvest festival, Misi Saljong (Sun god) is thanked for blessing people with a rich harvest. → Option A. UPPSC

In Tamil Nadu ﬁ rst three days of festival are dedicated to Goddess Lakshmi, next three days to Durga and last three to Saraswati.
